Boruto

the counter-example for P-007: Kara drops its unifier, then clashes

Contents

  1. Why it is in this library
  2. The power system in one paragraph
  3. Verified facts
  4. What the guide learned from it
  5. Contested or unverified
  6. Corrections to the sources

Why it is in this library

The source's counter-example for the visual test, set against Naruto's Akatsuki so that the same lineage supplies both the model and the failure.
It is the case where a villain group starts with a shared mark, on the presenter's reading stops wearing it, and is left with member designs that do not agree with each other.

The power system in one paragraph

Kara is the main antagonistic organisation in Boruto and it is structured in two tiers. The senior tier is the Inner, the wider tier the Outer. It is fronted by Jigen, whose body hosts Kara's founder and leader Isshiki Otsutsuki, and command passes to the Inner known as Code once Isshiki dies. The Inner are marked two ways, by a Roman numeral tattooed on the face and by the individual lining colour of the dark cloak they are introduced in, and many members, Inner and Outer alike, carry bodies implanted and modified with Scientific Ninja Tools, the work of Amado, the Inner scientist operating under Jigen. So the organisation has a rank system, a shared garment and a per-member mark, which is the full kit P-007 asks for. The question the source raises is what happens to a group's readability when the shared garment stops appearing and the per-member marks are all the eye has left.

Verified facts

FactVerdictSource tierReferenceCited by
Kara is the main antagonistic organisation in Boruto, structured with both Inner and Outer members; it is fronted by Jigen, whose body hosts founder and leader Isshiki Otsutsuki, and command passes to the Inner known as Code after Isshiki diesVERIFIEDSECONDARYhttps://gamerant.com/boruto-kara-explained/P-007
Kara has multiple members split into Inner and Outer ranks, and the Inner ranks are identified by distinctive Roman-numeral facial tattoos and unique coloured cloak liningsVERIFIEDSECONDARYhttps://gamerant.com/boruto-kara-explained/P-007
Every Kara Inner except Amado bears a Roman numeral tattooed on the face: Boro carries III and Code carries VI. What the numerals signify has not been revealedVERIFIEDFAN-WIKI-FETCHEDhttps://tvtropes.org/pmwiki/pmwiki.php/Characters/BorutoKaraP-007
Many Kara members, Inner and Outer alike, have bodies implanted and modified with Scientific Ninja Tools, and Amado, the Inner scientist working for Kara under Jigen, is the one who turned Kara members into cyborgsVERIFIEDFAN-WIKI-FETCHEDhttps://tvtropes.org/pmwiki/pmwiki.php/Characters/BorutoKaraP-007
Mikio Ikemoto worked as Masashi Kishimoto's chief assistant on Naruto from 1999 to 2014 and, after it concluded, was chosen to illustrate the 2016 sequel Boruto: Naruto Next Generations, working alongside writer Ukyo KodachiVERIFIEDSECONDARYhttps://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mikio_Ikemotocontext only
In the source, three images are pasted beside a Kara member one at a time as the presenter names them, a harlequin jester with a cane, then a top-hatted Victorian gentleman in tails, then a brown leather belt, all three on screen together until the shot cutsVERIFIEDPRIMARYhttps://www.youtube.com/watch?v=n933HtgVd1UP-007
Boruto's greater emphasis on science is attributed to writer Ukyo Kodachi, whose physician father and science fiction role-playing games shaped the setting, rather than to elapsed time since NarutoCORRECTEDSECONDARYhttps://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BorutoP-007

Note on what is and is not established here. The observation P-007 leans on, that Kara stops wearing the cloaks, is not in this table: no source documents the cloaks being abandoned, so it is held below as the presenter's reading of the later art. What is verified is the uniform and the per-member marks, not the uniform's disappearance. The two facts about the Roman numerals and the cyborg modification come from TV Tropes, an open fan-edited wiki read directly during the ingest, so they are FAN-WIKI-FETCHED and sit alongside the Game Rant material rather than founding a rule on their own. The member the presenter jokes about is never named on screen, so the pasted-images fact records what the source shows and attaches it to no character.

What the guide learned from it

  • P-007: the shared garment is what does the group-level work. Kara has a rank structure, a lining-colour system and a per-member tattoo, and none of that is visible at the distance a reader takes in a crowd. Strip the cloak and the group has only individual marks left, which read as people rather than as a force.
  • P-007: distinguishing marks that resolve only close up cannot substitute for a unifier. Roman numerals on a face and lining colours inside a cloak separate members from each other, which is the second half of the test, and they do nothing for the first half.
  • P-007: Boruto is the useful counter-example precisely because it shares a lineage with the model case. Naruto's Akatsuki cloak and Kara's cloak start from the same idea, so the difference in outcome points at the execution rather than at genre or era.
  • P-007: silhouettes have to belong to one another as well as be distinct. The presenter's charge is that Kara's do not, and even taken as taste rather than as fact it names the failure a reader should test a draft for.

Contested or unverified

Everything in this section is what the presenter asserted. None of it is promoted to a principle page as fact.

  • That Kara members stop wearing the capes after the early part of the series. No source documents Kara abandoning the cloaks. They are recorded as the Inner uniform and appear mainly in early meeting scenes; whether they stop is his reading of the later art, not a recorded change. TV Tropes' Kara character page and Game Rant's Kara explainer both describe the uniform and neither describes it being dropped.
  • That with the capes gone the one thing holding the group's visual identity together becomes irrelevant. His judgement, following from the point above.
  • That Boruto is a masterclass in how not to do a villain organisation. Opinion, out of fact-check scope by design.
  • That taking over from Kishimoto is not a small ask and is difficult. Opinion.
  • That the Kara designs are messy and the eye does not know where to go. Opinion.
  • That one unnamed Kara member looks like a jester, a Victorian gentleman and a leather belt thrown together. Opinion, and the member is never named on screen or in the captions, so even the referent cannot be identified from this source.
  • That another Kara member has a flowy white top covered in tattoos. The member is not named on screen or in the caption track at that point and no name card is shown, so the individual cannot be identified and the description cannot be checked against canon. No named Kara member should be attached to this description without a shot-level identification.
  • That many fans see Kara as generic villains from another science fiction anime rather than as rogue ninja. Opinion, and a claim about fan reception that was not measured.
  • The presenter's reading of the member designs as overcomplicated hair and asymmetrical straps thrown all over the place. Held as taste, and not supported for Boro, who is described elsewhere as bald but for the crown of his head and dressed in a hooded robe over a coat.

Corrections to the sources

  • S-003 at 17:57. The presenter said Boruto leans heavily on technology because it happens years after Naruto. The cited page attributes the greater emphasis on science to writer Ukyo Kodachi, whose physician father and science fiction role-playing games shaped the setting, and it states no time gap between Naruto's ending and Boruto's setting. The "because" is his own inference. The anchor is wrong as well: Boruto opens at Naruto's own finale rather than years after it, and the gap he is reaching for sits between the Fourth Shinobi World War and Naruto's epilogue, not between Naruto and Boruto. Recorded so the causal version is never re-adopted.

See also

Demon Slayer

the counter-example for P-006: the Twelve Kizuki, a large villain roster whose members each leave the same mark

Contents

  1. Why it is in this library
  2. The power system in one paragraph
  3. Verified facts
  4. What the guide learned from it
  5. Contested or unverified
  6. Corrections to the sources

Why it is in this library

The counter-example the source pairs against Naruto's Akatsuki: a villain roster whose members are individually well built and structurally interchangeable.
It is the work the source uses to show that a group can spend real care on personality and backstory and still, on his reading, hand every member the same job in the heroes' story.

The power system in one paragraph

The Twelve Kizuki are not a power system but an organisation, and the structure is the point. They are the strongest demons under Muzan Kibutsuji, second in strength and authority only to him, split into six Upper Ranks and six Lower Ranks and numbered by strength, with Upper Rank One the strongest. Each member is under Muzan's direct command and has received a large portion of his blood, and rank is engraved into the eyes: a Lower Rank carries the number in one eye, an Upper Rank the number in one eye and the kanji for Upper Rank in the other. Muzan is himself a demon, outranks all twelve and is not a member, so the roster is a chain of command under one holder of power rather than a council of equals. The heroes meet its members one at a time, in rising order of rank.

Verified facts

FactVerdictSource tierReferenceCited by
The Twelve Kizuki are the strongest demons under Muzan Kibutsuji, six Upper Ranks and six Lower Ranks numbered by strength with Upper Rank One the strongest; Muzan is himself a demon, outranks all twelve and is not a memberCORRECTEDFAN-WIKI-FETCHEDhttps://kimetsu-no-yaiba.fandom.com/wiki/Twelve_KizukiP-006
The Twelve Kizuki are under Muzan's direct command, each having received a large portion of his blood; rank is engraved into the eyes, not tattooed, the number in one eye for a Lower Rank and the number plus the kanji for Upper Rank for an Upper RankVERIFIEDFAN-WIKI-FETCHEDhttps://kimetsu-no-yaiba.fandom.com/wiki/Twelve_KizukiP-006
Rui holds Lower Rank Five and is killed by Giyu; the demons Tanjiro met earlier, Yahaba and Susamaru, only falsely believed they were among the Twelve Kizuki, and Kyogai was a former Lower Rank Six whom Muzan had already stripped of his rankVERIFIEDSECONDARYhttps://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Demon_Slayer:_Kimetsu_no_Yaiba_charactersP-006
Rui gains Muzan's permission to turn other humans and demons into a replacement family with his blood, abuses them out of his warped view of familial bonds, and becomes envious of the Kamado siblings' bondVERIFIEDSECONDARYhttps://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Demon_Slayer:_Kimetsu_no_Yaiba_charactersP-006
Rui loses his calm demeanour and attacks erratically until Giyu kills him, so Tanjiro does not land the killing blowVERIFIEDSECONDARYhttps://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Demon_Slayer:_Kimetsu_no_Yaiba_charactersP-006
Gyutaro shares Upper Rank Six with his younger sister Daki and is given an extended tragic backstory: born in the red-light district slums and ostracised for his appearance, he worked as a debt collector and was mortally wounded after killing the customer who burned Daki alive, before Doma turned him into a demonVERIFIEDSECONDARYhttps://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Demon_Slayer:_Kimetsu_no_Yaiba_charactersP-006
Daki and Gyutaro hold Upper Rank Six jointly and must be decapitated together to be truly killed: Zenitsu and Inosuke kill Daki, and Tengen Uzui and Tanjiro kill GyutaroVERIFIEDSECONDARYhttps://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Demon_Slayer:_Kimetsu_no_Yaiba_charactersP-006
Hantengu holds Upper Rank Four and attacks the Swordsmith Village with Gyokko, where Tanjiro, Nezuko, Genya and Mitsuri confront him and Tanjiro kills him; his human past is not tragic, he was a prolific criminal who boasted of his innocence despite his conviction before Muzan turned him into a demonCORRECTEDSECONDARYhttps://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Demon_Slayer:_Kimetsu_no_Yaiba_charactersP-006

The fact check also recorded that Akaza, an Upper Rank, appears in the Mugen Train story between Rui and Gyutaro, so the run of elite demons the source walks through is not a strictly consecutive sequence.

What the guide learned from it

  • P-006: this is the counter-example, on the presenter's reading. Rui, Gyutaro and Hantengu each arrive as a harder wall, push the heroes past their limit, and are killed, and the shape of what each one does to the heroes' story does not change from one to the next. A member who could be swapped for the next member without changing the story has left no mark of his own.
  • P-006: individual quality does not rescue the pattern. Rui's warped substitute family and Gyutaro's slum childhood are built with real care, and the roster still reads as one repeated beat, which is why the principle is about the difference between members and not about how good any single member is.
  • P-006: Hantengu is the useful complication. His past is not tragic at all, so the work does vary the ingredient, and the structural job stays identical anyway. Varying the flavour of a member is not the same as varying what he costs the heroes.
  • P-006: the roster is also the scale warning P-005 makes from the other end. A chain of command with twelve numbered slots is a lot of slots to fill with distinct consequences.

Contested or unverified

Everything the source argues about the pattern here is criticism, not fact. Held as the presenter's position and never promoted.

  • He says he loves Demon Slayer but that it does this differently from the works he praises. His view.
  • He frames the Twelve Kizuki as supposed to be the greatest obstacle between the heroes and wiping out the demons. A superlative with no objective test, and his own words frame a design intent he then disputes. Held as his framing, not as a fact, and contestable anyway since Muzan outranks all twelve.
  • He holds that individually a lot of these demons are good villains with memorable personalities and backstories. Taste.
  • He holds that what each Kizuki contributes to the story is the same thing every time, and that the repeated beats are a harder challenge, hero growth, and a tragic reminder of lost humanity. This is the reading P-006 rests on. It is a critical argument about pattern, not a checkable claim.
  • He holds that making members the next wall for the heroes is fine but not memorable. Taste.

Corrections to the sources

  • S-003 said the Twelve Kizuki are the twelve strongest demons alive. That is true only of Muzan's subordinates: Muzan is himself a demon, outranks all twelve and is not a member. Never re-adopt the unrestricted form.
  • S-003 said Hantengu again has a tragic past. He does not. His human past is a prolific criminal who boasted of his innocence despite his conviction, so he is a deliberate exception to the sympathetic-demon pattern rather than another instance of it.

See also

P-005 A group of individuals, P-006 Every villain leaves a mark, Pavelover - How To Write A Villain Group (& How NOT To)

Fullmetal Alchemist

model for P-005, the Homunculi, one sin each

Contents

  1. Why it is in this library
  2. The power system in one paragraph
  3. Verified facts
  4. What the guide learned from it
  5. Contested or unverified
  6. Corrections to the sources

Why it is in this library

The model case for a villain group whose members stay individually legible: one organisation, one purpose, and a different deadly sin built into each body, so no two of them do the same job.
It is the designed version of the pattern, a cast manufactured to a plan rather than assembled from strays, which is why the roster still reads as people rather than a crowd.

The power system in one paragraph

The Homunculi are not an alliance, they are a manufacture. Father is the maker and the seven are his children, each one a living vessel for an appetite he cast off, each named for the sin it carries, and each meant to use its own gift to further the one plan. That gives the group its unit and its variety in the same stroke: the name is the character brief, so the member's method, its uses and the trouble it makes for the heroes all follow from the vice it was built out of. Two structural exceptions sit inside the design and are worth holding rather than smoothing over. Greed abandons Father's agenda, so the manufacture does not guarantee obedience. Wrath, Fuhrer King Bradley, is the exception to the method, a human who alone survived being injected with Father's Philosopher's Stone rather than being cast off from him.

Verified facts

FactVerdictSource tierReferenceCited by
The known Homunculi of Amestris were created by Father and are living storage units for his pride, greed, wrath, lust, sloth, envy and gluttony; his seven children are meant to use their unique gifts to further his overall planVERIFIEDSECONDARYhttps://www.cbr.com/fullmetal-alchemist-homunculus-explained/P-005
Every Homunculus except Father bears the name of one of the seven deadly sins: Lust, Gluttony, Envy, Greed, Wrath, Sloth and PrideVERIFIEDSECONDARYhttps://gamerant.com/fullmetal-alchemist-brotherhood-homunculi-powers-explained/P-005
Lust is the form Father took when he rid himself of his longing for human flesh; she uses her good looks to inspire lust in others, allowing her to control just about anyone, and she took an alias and tried to seduce Jean Havoc to find out what he knew about Father's plansVERIFIEDSECONDARYhttps://www.cbr.com/fullmetal-alchemist-lust-homunculus-powers/P-005
Fuhrer King Bradley is a superior swordsman whose ultimate eye is able to anticipate attacks, enabling him to dodge them, so his violence is anticipatory and precise rather than frenziedVERIFIEDSECONDARYhttps://www.cbr.com/fullmetal-alchemist-anime-characters-bradley-could-defeat-would-lose-against/P-005
Gluttony was designed not only to track but to dispose of evidence to keep Father's plans a secret: after Lust's squad dispatched Father Cornello he was tasked with eating him to erase all evidence of the death, and he was allowed to eat Ling YaoVERIFIEDSECONDARYhttps://www.cbr.com/fullmetal-alchemist-gluttony-weakest-homunculus/P-005

Scope note: every fact above holds for the manga and the 2009 Brotherhood continuity the source uses. In the 2003 anime the Homunculi come from failed human transmutations and serve Dante, so nothing on this page may be read across to that version.

What the guide learned from it

  • P-005: one maker, one plan, and a different discarded appetite in every body gives an organisation its unity by construction while keeping each member pickable out of a line-up. The sin is the brief, so the writer never has to remember who is who.
  • P-005: the design has to show up in behaviour, not only in the naming. Lust manipulates and seduces her way to what Father's enemies know, Wrath kills by anticipation and precision, Gluttony eats the evidence. Each vice is given work no other member of the group would be sent to do.
  • P-005: the guide's own read, not the source's, is that the two exceptions strengthen the case rather than dent it. A member who walks out on the plan and a member built by a different method are only possible in a group whose members are people first, which is the state the rule is asking for.

Contested or unverified

None yet.

Corrections to the sources

None yet.

See also

Gachiakuta

the fresh model case for P-001, with supporting evidence for P-002 and P-004. The source itself declines to judge P-003 progression against this work because the series is too new

Contents

  1. Why it is in this library
  2. The power system in one paragraph
  3. Verified facts
  4. What the guide learned from it
  5. Contested or unverified
  6. Corrections to the sources

Why it is in this library

The source's second worked "iconic powers" case: a junk-to-weapon transformation the source treats as instantly recognizable because nothing else in its comparison set looks like it.

The power system in one paragraph

Rudo's power, 3R, channels a Vital Instrument's worth of power into a discarded object, reshaping it into a weapon whose form is set by how Rudo reads the object's abandoned purpose. Use is capped by exhaustion rather than a clock: drawing roughly three Vital Instruments' worth of power at once, or empowering one object three times, leaves Rudo physically drained. A transformed object crumbles to dust after a short time.

Verified facts

FactVerdictSource tierReferenceCited by
Rudo's power can only be used on objects that have already been discarded or broken, never on something still owned or valuedVERIFIEDFAN-WIKI-UNFETCHEDhttps://gachiakuta.fandom.com/wiki/3RP-001
A transformed object reverts to dust after a short durationVERIFIEDFAN-WIKI-UNFETCHEDhttps://gachiakuta.fandom.com/wiki/3RP-004
Rudo's activation limit is a stamina cap per use, roughly three Vital Instruments' worth of power at once (or one object empowered three times), not a literal three-per-day limitCORRECTEDFAN-WIKI-UNFETCHEDhttps://gachiakuta.fandom.com/wiki/3RP-004
Rudo was thrown off a cliff into the Pit, falsely accused of killing RegtoVERIFIEDFAN-WIKI-UNFETCHEDhttps://gachiakuta.fandom.com/wiki/3RP-001
The item Rudo transforms into a defensive floating shield is Gris's "Lucky Charm," not Rudo's own, described as squid-like (five limbs, three joints each) rather than octopus-like; its interpreted purpose, protecting people, is accurateCORRECTEDFAN-WIKI-UNFETCHEDhttps://gachiakuta.fandom.com/wiki/Lucky_CharmP-002

Both wiki pages above returned HTTP 402 or 403 on direct fetch at fact-check time; their content is relayed through search-result summaries of those pages, not a direct read. Per this library's rule, neither fact may become the sole basis of a principle.

What the guide learned from it

  • P-001: a junk-to-weapon transformation reads as iconic because it is visually distinct from every other power in the source's comparison set, not because the underlying "transform an object" idea is new
  • P-002: the transformation is not a random toolbox; its form is fixed by how Rudo reads the object's discarded purpose, keeping one mechanism at the centre of the power
  • P-004: the power carries built-in limits on two axes at once, what it can be used on (discarded objects only) and how much it can be used before Rudo is physically drained, plus a short duration on every result

Contested or unverified

Gachiakuta's serialization start date (Weekly Shōnen Magazine, from 16 Feb 2022), its English-language license (Kodansha USA, announced March 2023, first volume slated Fall 2023), its anime adaptation (announced 13 June 2024, reported as Studio Bones), and its authorship credits (manga by Kei Urana, graffiti designs by Andō Hideyoshi): all come from the on-screen Fandom-style wiki card visible in S-001 at 00:07:18.8, and none were run through the fact-check pass. Held here as unverified rather than stated as fact until checked against Kodansha's own listing, per the fact-checker's own recommendation.

Corrections to the sources

The video states Rudo "can only activate three objects per day." Per the Gachiakuta Wiki's 3R page, the limit is a stamina cap on how much power can be drawn at once (roughly three Vital Instruments' worth, or one object empowered three times) before Rudo is left physically drained, not a 24-hour daily cap.

The video calls the item Rudo transforms an "octopus-looking shield" that is his own. Per the Gachiakuta Wiki, the item is Gris's "Lucky Charm," described as squid-like rather than octopus-like, and belongs to Gris, not Rudo.

Hunter x Hunter

model for P-005 (the Phantom Troupe as a group of individuals) and the lead example in P-007's shadow-group exception

Contents

  1. Why it is in this library
  2. The power system in one paragraph
  3. Verified facts
  4. What the guide learned from it
  5. Contested or unverified
  6. Corrections to the sources

Why it is in this library

The source's model case for a villain group built out of individuals: a fixed, small roster where every member is a person you could pick out of a line-up, tied together by one shared mark.
It is also the lead example in the source's shadow-group exception to P-007, and the one place the fact check turned that exception against the source.

The power system in one paragraph

This work is not in the library for a power system, it is here for an organisation. The Phantom Troupe, the Genei Ryodan, are a band of thieves, murderers and criminals who operate as a single outfit and are read by their world as a single outfit. The membership is small enough that each member gets time on the page, and every member carries a numbered spider tattoo that says which of them they are inside the group. That is the shape the source is arguing for: individually identifiable members, each with an ability and a silhouette of their own (a woman whose conjured vacuum cleaner swallows evidence, a bandaged fighter who reads as a mummy in boxing gloves), held together by one shared symbol that never flattens them into a crowd. The same fetched source notes that Hisoka never truly belonged to the Troupe, which matters to anyone counting who is actually in it.

Verified facts

FactVerdictSource tierReferenceCited by
The Phantom Troupe (Genei Ryodan) are a band of thieves, murderers and criminals in Hunter x Hunter, thirteen members, each marked with a numbered spider tattooVERIFIEDSECONDARYhttps://www.thegamer.com/hunter-x-hunter-phantom-troupe-explained-origin-story/P-005
Shizuku's conjured vacuum cleaner Blinky disposes of evidence that could lead back to the Troupe, and absorbs living as well as non-living thingsVERIFIEDSECONDARYhttps://gamerant.com/hunter-x-hunter-strongest-phantom-troupe-members-ranked/P-005
Bonolenov looks like a mummy with boxing gloves, the gloves and bandages concealing the holes in his bodyVERIFIEDSECONDARYhttps://gamerant.com/hunter-x-hunter-strongest-phantom-troupe-members-ranked/P-005
Hisoka never truly belonged to the TroupeVERIFIEDSECONDARYhttps://gamerant.com/hunter-x-hunter-strongest-phantom-troupe-members-ranked/P-005
The Phantom Troupe are infamous criminals feared worldwide, every member carrying a Class A bounty from the Hunter Association, and they share a spider tattooCORRECTEDSECONDARYhttps://www.cbr.com/hunter-x-hunter-phantom-troupe-explained/P-007

Every fact here comes from a fetched secondary page, so nothing on this page rests on an unfetched fan wiki.

What the guide learned from it

  • P-005: a villain group can be read as one force and still resolve into people, because the roster is small enough for each member to get a distinct ability, look and reason for being there. This is the positive half of the pairing the source builds against a larger, thinner roster.
  • P-005: the members are differentiated by what they carry as much as by how they fight, the vacuum cleaner and the bandages doing the work of a character sheet in one image.
  • P-007: the shared mark is the group. The numbered spider tattoo is the whole of what ties the Troupe together visually, no cloak and no uniform, and it is enough because each member's own silhouette is unmistakable inside it.
  • P-007: the fact check demoted this work from a clean exception to a qualified one. The exception now has to be argued on the absence of a uniform, not on the group being hidden, because it is not hidden. See Corrections to the sources.

Contested or unverified

  • The presenter's line that putting the Phantom Troupe in a room shows every one of them feeling completely different is his opinion, not a factual claim, and is carried here as his.
  • The presenter names a clown among the three members he picks out. No fetched page identifies any Troupe member as a clown. CBR compares Hisoka's design to the Auguste clown at https://www.cbr.com/hunter-x-hunter-hisoka-pennywise-character-design/, and all three of the members he names are visible in the video's own frames between 106 and 110 seconds, but the identification itself is unconfirmed and stays here.
  • The Japanese name Deme-chan, used in the source for the vacuum cleaner, is dropped: no fetched source carries it. The name verified for that ability is Blinky.

Corrections to the sources

  • S-003, Verification row 119. The presenter said the Phantom Troupe are a group operating in the shadows, and used them as the lead example of villain groups that can forgo a shared uniform because they are hidden. The cited page refutes that for its own lead example: the Troupe are infamous criminals feared worldwide, every member carries a Class A bounty from the Hunter Association, and they share a spider tattoo. They are not operating in the shadows and they do carry a shared mark. Never re-adopt the shadow framing for this work.

See also

P-005 A group of individuals, P-007 A group you can see, Pavelover - How To Write A Villain Group (& How NOT To)

Invincible

the model case for the faceless-collective exception in P-005 (the Viltrumites)

Contents

  1. Why it is in this library
  2. The power system in one paragraph
  3. Verified facts
  4. What the guide learned from it
  5. Contested or unverified
  6. Corrections to the sources

Why it is in this library

S-003's model for the one kind of villain group allowed to have no faces: an empire whose threat is its sameness rather than its cast.
It is the exception that keeps P-005 honest, because it shows a faceless collective working as a deliberate design rather than as a roster the writer ran out of time for.

The power system in one paragraph

The Viltrumites are not a crew, they are an empire: an interstellar conquering power whose warriors share the same physical class of ability and are sent out in small numbers because a small number is enough to take a planet. Wikipedia's Viltrum Empire entry gives the mechanism behind that sameness as a centuries-long eugenics programme and advanced genetic manipulation, with the Great Purge culling the population to rebuild what the empire regarded as a perfect civilisation. Structurally, then, the group is an ideology with bodies attached: rank and obedience carry it, not a set of named personalities, and the handful of Viltrumites the story does follow closely stand out against a mass rather than inside a line-up. Everything the source argues about how that mass is written, as opposed to how the empire is built in-world, is his reading and is held below.

Verified facts

FactVerdictSource tierReferenceCited by
Viltrumite uniformity comes from a centuries-long eugenics programme and advanced genetic manipulation, with the Great Purge removing roughly half the population to rebuild a perfect civilisationCORRECTEDSECONDARYhttps://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Viltrum_EmpireP-005

No fact about this work rests on an unfetched fan wiki. The gap is a different one and worth naming: the load-bearing point for P-005, that the facelessness is intended, has no verified fact behind it at all. It sits under Contested or unverified as the presenter's craft reading and is never promoted.

What the guide learned from it

  • P-005: the faceless collective is a legitimate exception to the individuals rule when sameness is the point of the group, and the Viltrumites are the source's model case for it
  • P-005: canon supplies a designed-in reason for the sameness, a eugenics programme and the Great Purge, so the exception can be argued from how the group is built in-world rather than from how many members the writer had time for
  • P-005: the presenter's stronger claims, that most Viltrumites get little development and were not meant to stand out, did not survive checking, so the rule takes the shape of the exception from this work and not the assertion of intent

Contested or unverified

  • The presenter asserts that most individual Viltrumites do not get much development. That is his craft reading, not a sourced fact: no source found states it, "much development" has no defined threshold, so "most" cannot be tested, and the CBR Viltrumite culture article read during the fact check covers origins, the Great Purge, conquest strategy and powers, never how many Viltrumites receive characterisation.
  • The presenter asserts that the Viltrumites are not all supposed to stand out as unique characters. That is a claim about authorial intent, carried as his reading: no Robert Kirkman statement was found saying the warriors were designed not to stand out as individuals. Canon does show a rigidly uniform, obedient warrior culture, which is a different claim.
  • The presenter asserts that the Viltrumites are built to feel like an unstoppable force. Craft assertion, not a checkable fact, carried as his argument.

Corrections to the sources

  • S-003 says every Viltrumite was born and raised in the same way so that all of them would become weapons. Wikipedia's Viltrum Empire entry gives the mechanism as a centuries-long eugenics programme and advanced genetic manipulation, with the Great Purge, so identical upbringing alone is not what the source describes and the video's version is an overstatement. A culling of weak infants was once attached to this claim in checking and is not canon either: the page cited for it describes no such programme, only the Great Purge civil war, so that detail and that citation are dropped. Recorded here so neither version is re-adopted.

See also

Jujutsu Kaisen

the model case for P-004: an ability-limitation drawback (Yuji Itadori's Divergent Fist), with Gojo Satoru held as the series' flawless-seeming aside

Contents

  1. Why it is in this library
  2. The power system in one paragraph
  3. Verified facts
  4. What the guide learned from it
  5. Contested or unverified
  6. Corrections to the sources

Why it is in this library

S-002's second model case for a permanent drawback type, one built into the ability itself rather than the body or the mind, plus Gojo Satoru's Domain Expansion, already cited by S-001 as a signature-move example for P-001.

The power system in one paragraph

Jujutsu sorcerers channel cursed energy into named techniques. Yuji Itadori's Divergent Fist is not a trained technique at all but an unintentional side effect of cursed energy lagging behind his physical strikes, later refined into a deliberate move; Mahito's Idle Transfiguration reshapes souls, his own or via touch on others, altering the body's form. Gojo Satoru's Domain Expansion, Unlimited Void, is cited by S-001 as a signature-move example of an iconic power; S-002 names Gojo only in passing, as the series' apparently flawless character, and never names the technique.

Verified facts

FactVerdictSource tierReferenceCited by
Yuji's Divergent Fist arose because his cursed energy lagged behind his physical strikes; later refined into a deliberate techniqueVERIFIEDFAN-WIKI-UNFETCHEDhttps://jujutsu-kaisen.fandom.com/wiki/Divergent_FistP-004
Mahito's Idle Transfiguration reshapes souls, his own or via touch on others, altering the body's form accordinglyVERIFIEDFAN-WIKI-UNFETCHEDhttps://jujutsu-kaisen.fandom.com/wiki/Idle_TransfigurationP-004
Gojo Satoru's Domain Expansion is Unlimited Void, one of the series' most iconic techniquesVERIFIEDFAN-WIKI-FETCHEDhttps://jujutsu-kaisen.fandom.com/wiki/Domain_Expansion-

What the guide learned from it

  • P-004: Yuji's Divergent Fist is the source's clearest example of a permanent cost built into the ability's own limits rather than into the body or the mind, fought against a shapeshifting opponent to sharpen the contrast
  • P-004: Gojo is named twice by S-002, once in passing as apparently flawless and once in the conclusion as "at their absolute coolest when you see the flaws in their powers," an opinion (S-002 Verification row 28) rather than a checkable fact, held here as the series' own flawless-seeming aside rather than promoted to a rule

None yet.

None yet.

My Hero Academia

the paired model for P-003, run directly against Solo Leveling to isolate time, not merely a drawback's presence, as the real variable behind earned progression

Contents

  1. Why it is in this library
  2. The power system in one paragraph
  3. Verified facts
  4. What the guide learned from it
  5. Contested or unverified
  6. Corrections to the sources

Why it is in this library

S-002 pairs Deku directly against Sung Jin-Woo, a character who also starts as a weakling, to argue the deciding factor is not whether a drawback exists but how long the story makes the character pay it before the power-up lands.

The power system in one paragraph

Izuku "Deku" Midoriya is given One For All by All Might, the world's Symbol of Peace and the power's 8th holder. Deku's body cannot initially withstand it: early use breaks his own bones, forcing him to improvise costly, self-damaging workarounds, breaking his own fingers for a shockwave attack, before he gradually learns to control it and unlocks further One For All vestige abilities.

Verified facts

FactVerdictSource tierReferenceCited by
All Might, the world's Symbol of Peace, is the 8th holder of One For All and passes it to Izuku MidoriyaVERIFIEDFAN-WIKI-UNFETCHEDhttps://myheroacademia.fandom.com/wiki/Toshinori_YagiP-003
Even after ten months of training, Deku's body could not handle One For All, breaking almost all his bones on early full-power useVERIFIEDSECONDARYhttps://www.cbr.com/dekus-most-painful-broken-bones/P-003
In the Sports Festival fight against Todoroki, Deku breaks his own fingers repeatedly for Delaware Smash shockwaves, eventually sacrificing his whole left arm for a full-power Smash; Recovery Girl later notes permanent scarring from the repeated fracturesVERIFIEDFAN-WIKI-UNFETCHEDhttps://myheroacademia.fandom.com/wiki/Izuku_Midoriya_vs._Shoto_TodorokiP-003
Deku later gains further One For All vestige quirks: Blackwhip (dark energy tendrils), Smokescreen and Float (flight)VERIFIEDSECONDARYhttps://gamerant.com/my-hero-academia-one-for-all-quirks/P-003
My Hero Academia's anime run is 170 episodes totalVERIFIEDSECONDARYhttps://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/My_Hero_Academia_season_8P-003

What the guide learned from it

  • P-003: Deku vs Todoroki is the source's second worked example for earned progression, paired directly against Solo Leveling's Sung Jin-Woo, see the Solo Leveling case study, to argue the deciding factor is time spent paying a visible cost, not simply whether a drawback exists at all

Contested or unverified

The source's specific framing, "over 150 episodes" for Deku against "less than 20 episodes" for Jin-Woo, is not a defined, checkable milestone in either series (S-002 Verification row 21). This page carries only the total confirmed episode run, not the video's own comparison figure, and the comparison itself is not adopted as fact on any principle page.

Corrections to the sources

None yet.

Naruto

the model case for P-004 (a structural physical-cost drawback and the rotation pattern), and now also the model case for P-006 and P-007 (the Akatsuki)

Contents

  1. Why it is in this library
  2. The power system in one paragraph
  3. Verified facts
  4. What the guide learned from it
  5. Contested or unverified
  6. Corrections to the sources

Why it is in this library

The source's first model case for a permanent, structural drawback, and a second work, alongside One Piece, evidencing that a drawback rotates onto a new cost rather than disappearing as a character grows stronger.
It is also the library's model villain group: S-003 runs the Akatsuki through two of its three tests, the mark each member leaves on the heroes' story (P-006) and the shared visual identity that each member's own silhouette still stands out inside (P-007).

The power system in one paragraph

Most Naruto characters fight with ninjutsu and genjutsu; Rock Lee has neither, and instead masters taijutsu and the Eight Gates, a set of body points that, opened in sequence, grant escalating speed and power at an escalating physical cost. Naruto Uzumaki's own late-series forms layer a second system on top: a chain of transformations, each unlocked at a different cost, loss of control, mandatory training, a risk of failure, or the form's own aftermath.

The organisation S-003 studies is the Akatsuki: a criminal group mainly composed of rogue ninja (missing-nin) drawn from several villages, most of them S-ranked, Kage-level shinobi. Its stated operation is hunting the nine Tailed Beasts to reform the Ten-Tails, part of the Eye of the Moon Plan to cast Infinite Tsukuyomi over all humanity. On screen the roster is held together by one shared black robe with red clouds, though the robe is not worn across the whole run, and one member is not serving the stated plan at all: Black Zetsu is the manifestation of Kaguya Otsutsuki's will and betrays Madara at the climax.

Verified facts

FactVerdictSource tierReferenceCited by
Rock Lee cannot use ninjutsu or genjutsu; trained by Might Guy into a taijutsu specialistVERIFIEDFAN-WIKI-UNFETCHEDhttps://naruto.fandom.com/wiki/Rock_LeeP-004

What the guide learned from it

  • P-004: Rock Lee is the source's clearest example of a body-type permanent cost, escalating with every gate opened and ending, at the extreme, in death
  • P-004: Naruto's chain of late-series forms corroborates the rotation pattern already read from One Piece's Gears, each new form retiring the last form's specific cost while carrying a cost of its own
  • P-006: the Akatsuki are the model for one member, one mark. Itachi bends Sasuke's whole arc and then reverses it, Pain forces Naruto's reckoning with the cycle of hatred, Zetsu turns the plot itself, and no two of those are the same kind of change
  • P-006: the corrections make the pattern stronger, not weaker. The Itachi reveal is not one clean chapter and did not spare anyone suffering, and it still leaves a mark no other member leaves
  • P-007: the black robe with red clouds is the shared mark that makes the roster read as one force, while Zetsu's plant biology, Kisame's shark features, Hidan's scythe and Sasori's puppet body keep every silhouette distinct inside it
  • P-007: the robe is not worn for the whole run, which shows the shared mark is a device a story can drop

Contested or unverified

The source's deep-pass frames matched to two of Naruto's late-series forms, a bandaged, red-eyed Nine-Tails-avatar face and a large fiery silhouette, read as consistent with a tailed-beast-cloak-style transformation, but the fact check verifies the general cost pattern (Sage Mode, Baryon Mode) rather than confirming which named form each specific frame shows. Held here rather than asserted.

From S-003, held as the presenter's assertions and never promoted:

  • That the Akatsuki have been doing purpose better than almost everybody in anime history, and that they are still considered one of the greatest villain organisations in anime.
  • That not a single one of the Akatsuki is doing the same thing as another, and that they had members just as wildly different as another group's and still felt like a group. This is the craft argument P-006 and P-007 are built on, not a checkable fact.
  • That the Pain confrontation completely changes Naruto as a character moving forward. The checkable plot facts underneath it are narrower and sit in the table above.
  • That the Zetsu reveal was the worst plot twist in anime.
  • That Naruto is the anime that cooked with visual design, that the cloak's genius is its simplicity, intimidating but discreet with the red clouds as the warning, that every member is physically different yet instantly recognisable, that a single red cloud still makes many fans think of the Akatsuki years later, that Kishimoto wrote these characters incredibly well, and that character design is one of the biggest reasons the Akatsuki are legendary.
  • The episode figure on the on-screen chapter and episode card matches no documented milestone and could not be pinned to one, so it stays the video's own unsourced count and is not a fact of record.

Corrections to the sources

  • S-003 said Itachi chose to be the villain in everyone's eyes so nobody had to suffer. He did create the misconception deliberately, but "so nobody had to suffer" is wrong: the clan was wiped out and Sasuke carried the burden for years.
  • S-003 said every Akatsuki member wears the same black cloak with red clouds. Not universal: the remaining members stopped wearing the robe after Nagato and Konan died, and Sasori wore it over Hiruko, the puppet he hid inside, rather than on his own body. The reading of the red clouds as the bloodshed on the Hidden Rain Village's soil is the cited article's own, not stated canon.
  • S-003 said Zetsu is just a plant. He is the Akatsuki's only nonhuman member and is built on plant biology, an extension of the Shinju, which is a different claim.
  • The source record had the on-screen chapter and episode card held across two shots. It persists across at least nine, and its window runs to about 602.8s rather than 602.5s.

See also

One Piece

the model case for P-001, P-002, P-003, P-004

Contents

  1. Why it is in this library
  2. The power system in one paragraph
  3. Verified facts
  4. What the guide learned from it
  5. Contested or unverified
  6. Corrections to the sources

Why it is in this library

The source's primary "iconic" case and its primary progression-and-flaws case: a single power, Luffy's rubber body, carried across all four principles as the model.

The power system in one paragraph

Luffy ate the Gum-Gum Fruit, a Devil Fruit that turns his body to rubber. The source's argument is that this one simple mechanism is reapplied in escalating, self-costly forms, Gear Second, Gear Third, Gear Fourth, Gear Fifth, each unlocked at a specific, far-apart point in the story and each carrying its own drawback.

Verified facts

FactVerdictSource tierReferenceCited by
Luffy loses the ability to swim (and all strength in water) as the standard rule for Devil Fruit users; he ate the Gum-Gum FruitVERIFIEDSECONDARYhttps://www.thedigitalfix.com/one-piece/why-cant-devil-fruit-users-swimP-004
Gear Second first appears at episode 272 ("Luffy is in Sight! Gather at the Courthouse Plaza"), covering chapters 385-387, versus CP9's BluenoVERIFIEDFAN-WIKI-FETCHEDhttps://onepiece.fandom.com/wiki/Episode_272P-003
Gear Second costs Luffy visible strain: steam from rapid blood flow, paralysis after extended use, and a warning it can shorten his lifespanVERIFIEDFAN-WIKI-FETCHEDhttps://onepiece.fandom.com/wiki/Gomu_Gomu_no_Mi/Gear_2_TechniquesP-004
Gear Third shrinks Luffy to child size for roughly as long as he used it, until he trains past the weakness post-timeskipVERIFIEDFAN-WIKI-FETCHEDhttps://onepiece.fandom.com/wiki/Gomu_Gomu_no_Mi/Gear_3_TechniquesP-004
Gear Fourth ("Boundman") first appears at episode 726, "Gear 4! The Phenomenal Boundman!" (aired 17 Jan 2016), against Doflamingo in the Dressrosa arcVERIFIEDFAN-WIKI-FETCHEDhttps://onepiece.fandom.com/wiki/Episode_726P-003
Gear Fourth leaves Luffy unable to use Haki for around ten minutes afterward, on top of severe stamina drainVERIFIEDFAN-WIKI-FETCHEDhttps://onepiece.fandom.com/wiki/Gomu_Gomu_no_Mi/Gear_4_TechniquesP-004
Gear Fifth first appears at episode 1071 (aired 6 Aug 2023), the first canon animated Gear 5, against KaidoVERIFIEDFAN-WIKI-FETCHEDhttps://onepiece.fandom.com/wiki/Episode_1071P-003
Gear Fifth visibly ages and drains Luffy after its time limit endsVERIFIEDSECONDARYhttps://gamerant.com/one-piece-biggest-drawbacks-gear-5th/P-004
The "sand," "electricity god," and "mochi" opponents the source describes Luffy beating with one ability are Crocodile, Enel, and KatakuriVERIFIEDFAN-WIKI-FETCHEDhttps://onepiece.fandom.com/wiki/Whole_Cake_Island_ArcP-002
Luffy's rubber body, the Gomu Gomu no Mi, was later revealed during the Kaido fight to have originally been the Hito Hito no Mi, Model: Nika, a Mythical Zoan renamed by the World Government to hide its true natureVERIFIEDFAN-WIKI-UNFETCHEDhttps://onepiece.fandom.com/wiki/Gomu_Gomu_no_MiP-004
Gear Third's shrinking drawback eases as Luffy grows stronger: later chapters and episodes show the side effect diminishing, on top of the original portrayal already carried in this tableVERIFIEDFAN-WIKI-UNFETCHEDhttps://wiki.animeprinted.com/glossary/one-piece/luffy-gearsP-004

What the guide learned from it

  • P-001: a familiar power idea, stretching, read as iconic because of how it is staged and improvised, not because the underlying ability is new
  • P-002: one mechanism (a rubber body) solved three unrelated fight problems (sand, electricity, mochi) through creative reapplication rather than a swapped toolkit
  • P-003: each Gear form arrived after a measured, escalating cost in story time (episode and chapter counts that are each independently checkable), making the power-up read as earned rather than handed to the character
  • P-004: every Gear form carries its own drawback, and while some earlier costs ease as he grows (Gear Third's shrinking is trained past post-timeskip), the source's point is that something always remains: a lost swim ability, physical strain, a lost Haki window, visible aging
  • P-004 (S-002): the Gear Third fade-with-growth detail is the concrete, checkable instance behind S-002's sharper reading of the rule, that the specific cost may change while the character never reaches a fully costless ceiling; the Gomu Gomu no Mi to Hito Hito no Mi retcon is new context S-001 did not carry, though S-002 does not use it as part of its own drawback argument

Contested or unverified

The source's deep-pass frames at 00:04:09 (a silhouetted grey-haired figure) and 00:04:42 (a blue/grey-skinned character with striped hair and orange projectiles) were not confidently matched to a specific character at the available video resolution. The fact check confirms which three opponents the "sand, electricity, mochi" line as a whole refers to (Crocodile, Enel, Katakuri), but does not confirm which of those two specific frames shows which fighter. Held here rather than asserted.

Corrections to the sources

None yet.

Solo Leveling

the counter-example for P-001, P-002, P-003 and P-004: an ability set the source treats as powerful but symptomatically unmemorable

Contents

  1. Why it is in this library
  2. The power system in one paragraph
  3. Verified facts
  4. What the guide learned from it
  5. Contested or unverified
  6. Corrections to the sources

Why it is in this library

The source's negative case across all four principles: not iconic, not simple, its progression compressed rather than earned, and its late fights carrying no real drawback.

The power system in one paragraph

Jinwoo fights primarily with dagger skills and a summoned Shadow Army (Shadow Extraction), backed by a game-like levelling System, a mage-type shadow casting ranged fire spells, dagger throw-and-recall via Ruler's Hand, superhuman speed and strength, and, per the source's account of one fight, invisibility as a last resort; drained resources are typically restored with a mana potion.

Verified facts

FactVerdictSource tierReferenceCited by
Solo Leveling (A-1 Pictures) won Anime of the Year at the 9th Crunchyroll Anime Awards (2025), the first manhwa adaptation to win the categoryVERIFIEDSECONDARYhttps://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/9th_Crunchyroll_Anime_AwardsP-001
Season 1 ran 12 episodes (Jan-Mar 2024) and Season 2 ran 13 episodes (Jan-Mar 2025): 25 episodes totalVERIFIEDSECONDARYhttps://screenrant.com/solo-leveling-strongest-hunter-jinwoo-liu-zhigang-explainer/P-003
By the Season 2 finale, Jinwoo reaches Level 100 and surpasses Korea's and Japan's S-Ranks, but the finale introduces Liu Zhigang ("strongest in Asia"), and the wider setting still places Thomas Andre above him as the world's strongest hunterCORRECTEDSECONDARYhttps://screenrant.com/solo-leveling-strongest-hunter-jinwoo-liu-zhigang-explainer/P-003
Jinwoo's confirmed ability set: Shadow Extraction/Shadow Army, Dagger Rush with recall via Ruler's Hand, the shadow Tusk casting AoE fire (Blazing Fire, Hellfire), and mana potions as a standard System itemVERIFIEDFAN-WIKI-FETCHEDhttps://solo-leveling.fandom.com/wiki/Sung_JinwooP-002
Jinwoo begins as the lowest-ranked, E-Rank hunter, nicknamed "humanity's weakest weapon," near-death against low-tier dungeon monstersVERIFIEDFAN-WIKI-UNFETCHEDhttps://solo-leveling.fandom.com/wiki/Sung_JinwooP-003
The System chose Jinwoo as its sole Player: only he can see or use it, and it gives him a level-up mechanic no other hunter hasVERIFIEDFAN-WIKI-UNFETCHEDhttps://solo-leveling.fandom.com/wiki/SystemP-004
Season 2 episode 6: Jinwoo defeats the Kargalgan-led high-orc army, whose spells "didn't have much effect," and he "hardly break[s] a sweat"VERIFIEDSECONDARYhttps://screenrant.com/solo-leveling-season-2-best-fights-ranked/P-004
As the Ant King, Beru drops S-Rank hunters in one or two hits; Jinwoo is immune to the Ant King's deadliest poison and resurrects Beru as a shadow soldier after defeating himVERIFIEDFAN-WIKI-UNFETCHEDhttps://solo-leveling.fandom.com/wiki/BeruP-004

What the guide learned from it

  • P-001: a large, borrowed-looking ability list (dagger skills, a summoned army) reads as familiar rather than iconic when no single element is redesigned as unique to this protagonist
  • P-002: many discrete abilities used on a next-available-tool basis, rather than one mechanism reapplied, is why the source reads the late fights as less tense
  • P-003: a real, checkable 25-episode run covers a jump the source frames as compressed rather than earned; canon's own hierarchy (Liu Zhigang, Thomas Andre) means the "strongest hunter" framing does not hold as stated, see Corrections to the sources
  • P-004: a chain of fallback abilities, mana potion, melee, ranged magic, invisibility, that always has an answer removes the sense of a persisting flaw
  • P-004 (S-002): a second, more granular pass over the same counter-example, naming the orc fight and Beru specifically as costless wins, and the System's level-up mechanic as the source of Jinwoo's escalating power

Contested or unverified

The source's precise "20 episodes" figure for the weakest-to-strongest jump was not independently checked against the show's own episode-by-episode power benchmarks; only the season totals (12 and 13 episodes) were verified. Held here rather than promoted to a principle page.

S-002's Red Gate ice-boss fight claim (Season 2, episodes 1-2): Jinwoo's Ice Elves opponent, led by Baruka, and his use of shadow soldiers Igris and a revived Iron are corroborated, but no source found confirms the specific "invisibility" or "no damage taken" mechanics the video describes. Held here rather than promoted to the Verified facts table.

Corrections to the sources

The video states that by the end of season 2, Jinwoo "is the strongest," full stop. Per a breakdown of the Season 2 finale, Jinwoo surpasses Korea's and Japan's S-Ranks, but the finale introduces Liu Zhigang as "strongest in Asia," and the wider setting still places Thomas Andre above him as the world's strongest hunter. The video's unqualified "strongest" claim is not accurate as of the Season 2 finale.

A-001 Kevin and Kuga against the four power tests

Kevin Young, Kūga (Hakai)

Contents

  1. Findings
  2. Open questions for Bupe
  3. Continuity hooks
  4. Canon dependencies
  5. Canon changes proposed

Findings

Eighteen findings, run against the four principles drawn from S-001, refreshed on 2026-09-04 after S-002 extended P-004 and strengthened P-003. Verdict tokens are PASSES, AT-RISK and UNRESOLVED. AT-RISK means canon does not currently answer what the principle asks, never that a locked call is wrong. The extended P-004 sorts a cost by where it sits, on the body, on the ability's own limits, or on the mind, and asks whether it has been paid where an audience can watch it. The strengthened P-003 asks how much time the audience watches a climb take. Six rows were added on those two lenses and no existing verdict moved, because canon has not changed since this page was first written.

Open questions for Bupe

Ten choices. Nothing here carries a recommendation, and the affected findings do not move until Bupe rules.

Q1. Does Kevin get a signature visual, and if so what kind?

Why canon forces it: P-001 asks for one image that tells you whose the power is, and canon has the concept half without the image half.
Options:

  1. (a) No signature move at all: the power's whole identity is Kūga's colour readout, and Kevin is the boy the colour is read off.
  2. (b) A signature visual that is not a move: one repeatable image for a declaration landing (for example the colour flooding Kūga in gold, held for one beat), used identically every time from the first use to the finale.
  3. (c) A named technique in the shonen sense, invented later in the run and earned.
  4. (d) Park it until Episode 6, and let the name reveal carry the visual too.

Status: OPEN

Q2. Is Kevin on the tier ladder or off it?

Why canon forces it: the Bible says both, and P-002 is failed by a protagonist carrying two power systems.
Options:

  1. (a) Off it entirely: retire "reaches 2 (Awakened) by end of Season 1" from the Bible stat block and the profile, and Kevin's only measure is capacity.
  2. (b) On it as well: Kevin trains ordinary Aura like everyone else and separately carries Kūga, so he has two systems.
  3. (c) On it nominally: he awakens Tier 2 Aura because he is Jaden's brother and lineage says he would have anyway, and the two systems never interact.

Status: OPEN

Q3. What is the audience-visible unit of "capacity"?

Why canon forces it: P-003 needs the bill watched being paid, and capacity is an internal quantity in a visual medium.
Options:

  1. (a) Kūga's colour readout is promoted to the official meter: how much colour, how long it holds, how fast it drains.
  2. (b) A physical cost meter: what it does to Kevin's body when he holds it, escalating and then receding as capacity grows.
  3. (c) Duration, stated in the writing: a flicker, then a second, then a sentence, then a decision.
  4. (d) Nothing external: capacity is only ever shown by what he manages to want, and the audience infers it.

Status: OPEN

Q4. When does the audience get taught the declaration rule?

Why canon forces it: P-002's tension only exists once the audience knows what the power can do.
Options:

  1. (a) Early and explicitly, from Kūga's mouth, once, so every later scene can play against it.
  2. (b) Demonstrated three times before it is ever stated, so the audience works it out (the parent Writing Law, "The reader works it out. You do not tell them").
  3. (c) Stated at the Episode 6 correction, as part of the same beat as the name.

Status: OPEN

Q5. Does Kūga speak in Season 1 before Episode 6?

Why canon forces it: canon puts the full conversation in Season 2 and Stage 1 at single words, and through three shipped episodes he has said nothing.
Options:

  1. (a) Single words only, starting now, one or two an episode.
  2. (b) Silence until Episode 6, so the correction is his first line and it detonates.
  3. (c) Single words from Episode 4, and the test pattern ("I could just...", "Oh. Interesting.") arrives before the name.

Status: OPEN

Q6. After the Season 3 twist, does the asymmetry survive?

Why canon forces it: P-004 requires a flaw that changes without vanishing, and canon is silent on the after-state.
Options:

  1. (a) It survives: Kūga now knows love as an input but good stays expensive, so the flaw changed and did not vanish.
  2. (b) It inverts: after the twist harm becomes the expensive one, which is a new flaw rather than no flaw.
  3. (c) It resolves: the asymmetry was the arc and the arc is over. On (c) the protagonist ends with no permanent limit, and the craft lens would flag it.

Status: OPEN

Q7. How old is Kevin at the Season 3 finale, and is the manhood deadline met or beaten?

Why canon forces it: the deadline is fixed mechanically and the finale age is never stated.
Options:

  1. (a) He beats the deadline: still a boy at the finale, and the shape sets because he set it.
  2. (b) He reaches it: the finale IS the deadline, and the twist happens at the exact moment the window closes.
  3. (c) The deadline passes unresolved and Season 3 is not the end of it.

Status: OPEN

Q8. Is the Scrapbook "mimicked stance" beat the first-power moment or a separate beat?

Why canon forces it: the profile carries it as a candidate for both the first power act and the training trigger, and P-003 wants the power beat on an emotional peak.
Options:

  1. (a) Yes: Kevin's first power act is copying Jaden's stance without knowing why, seen by Jon, and it doubles as the training trigger.
  2. (b) No: keep it as a pure mimicry beat with no Kūga involvement, so the first declaration lands later and cleanly.
  3. (c) Both, separated by episodes.

Status: OPEN

Q9. Which kind of cost does the audience meet first, and in which beat?

Why canon forces it: canon prices Kevin on the body, on the ability itself and on the mind, and through three shipped episodes not one of the three has been paid where an audience could watch it.
Options:

  1. (a) The mind first, through the intrusive-thought mechanic, so the audience's first lesson is that Kevin's own head is the dangerous part.
  2. (b) The body first, through holding a flicker and nearly dying of it, so the manhood deadline becomes physically real early.
  3. (c) The ability's own limit first, through a declaration that fails because a tremor of doubt was in it, so the audience learns the rule by watching it refuse him.
  4. (d) All three inside one near-catastrophe, so the price is taught once and completely and never has to be re-explained.

Status: OPEN

Q10. When does the audience learn that Kūga dies if Kevin dies?

Why canon forces it: that tether is Kūga's only permanent limit, and the locked reveal order places the name, the mechanic and the origin while saying nothing about this one.
Options:

  1. (a) With the Ultimatum in Season 2, in the same conversation, so the threat and the leash arrive together.
  2. (b) Earlier, as a Kūga boast that reads as a threat and only later reads as a confession.
  3. (c) At the finale, so every earlier scene where he kept Kevin alive replays with a different motive.
  4. (d) Never stated: the audience only ever infers it from what he refuses to let happen to Kevin.

Status: OPEN

Continuity hooks

One line per obligation this page creates, tagged so a director can grep the tag and get every craft obligation on that character or arc.

  • CHAR:Kevin ARC:S1 If Kevin gets a signature visual (Q1), it must be identical from its first use to the Episode 6 name reveal.
  • CHAR:Kevin SYSTEM:Aura The tier line and the stated exemption from the tier scale cannot both stand (Q2); whichever Bupe keeps is fixed in the Series Bible stat block and the Kevin Young profile in the same edit.
  • CHAR:Kuga SYSTEM:PowerOfWill If the colour readout becomes the capacity meter (Q3), every amplification beat obeys it from its first use onward.
  • CHAR:Kevin SYSTEM:PowerOfWill The declaration rule has to reach the audience before any scene can play against it (Q4).
  • CHAR:Kuga ARC:S1 EP:S1E06 Kuga's first spoken line is a placement call (Q5); the Episode 6 correction stays the beat that carries the name either way.
  • CHAR:Kevin ARC:S1 EP:S1E06 The name reveal stays gated on a power act, never on the episode number.
  • CHAR:Kevin ARC:S3 If the asymmetry is retired at the Final Twist (Q6), the after-state needs a stated permanent limit or P-004 stops being met; under the extended rule a cost that changes as Kevin grows still counts, and only a cost that vanishes fails.
  • CHAR:Kevin ARC:S3 Kevin's age at the finale is unstated (Q7) while the manhood deadline is mechanical, so the two are settled together.
  • CHAR:Kevin ARC:S1 The first-power moment and the Jon training trigger are one open beat (Q8) until Bupe separates them.
  • CHAR:Kevin ARC:S1 Through three shipped episodes Kevin has used no power and Kuga has spoken no line; any first use lands against that silence.
  • CHAR:Kevin SYSTEM:PowerOfWill Canon prices Kevin on the body, on the ability and on the mind, and none of the three has been paid where an audience can see it; the first payment (Q9) is what teaches them the price is real.
  • CHAR:Kevin ARC:S1 ARC:S3 Kevin has no form ladder, so no later power-up can retire an old cost; any named form introduced later contradicts "neither number moves".
  • CHAR:Kuga SYSTEM:PowerOfWill The tether that kills Kuga with Kevin has no reveal placement (Q10) while the name, the mechanic and the origin all have one.

A-002 The ensemble against the four power tests

the Aura system, Voodoo, Face (Devon Jakes), Jon (Jonathan Clark, Blade), Jaden Young (Daps), Donte Jakes (Blaze), Uno, Tribal, Sera, Trace, the ensemble as a whole

Contents

  1. Findings
  2. Open questions for Bupe
  3. Continuity hooks
  4. Canon dependencies
  5. Canon changes proposed

Findings

Two systems, eight named subjects and the ensemble as a whole, each run against the four principles, plus the five open differentiation flags. Verdict tokens are PASSES, AT-RISK and UNRESOLVED. AT-RISK means canon does not currently answer what the principle asks, never that a locked call is wrong. Three of the four principles are written for a protagonist and P-002 carries an explicit carve-out for villains and side characters, so a kit is scored against that carve-out rather than against the protagonist bar. The differentiation rows run against P-001, because a power that blurs into another power is a power no single image identifies. Part 3's sixth pair, Manic and Tribal on memory, is recorded in canon as already differentiated and carries no row. Refreshed 2026-09-04 after S-002 extended P-004 and strengthened P-003: the extended P-004 sorts a cost by where it sits, on the body, on the ability's own limits, or on the mind, and asks whether it has been paid where an audience can watch it, and the strengthened P-003 asks how much time the audience watches a climb take. Thirteen rows were added on those two lenses and no existing verdict moved, because canon has not changed since this page was first written.

Open questions for Bupe

Fifteen choices. Nothing here carries a recommendation, and the affected findings do not move until Bupe rules.

Q1. Jaden has no flaw. What is the cost of being the most complete ninja?

Why canon forces it: P-004 asks for a limit that stays, and canon gives Jaden no cost, no drain and nothing he cannot do.
Options:

  1. (a) The adaptation is prediction, so a genuinely novel opponent blinds him and he is briefly ordinary.
  2. (b) The suppression is no longer a choice: seven years of never going full has cost him the top of his range, and he does not know it yet.
  3. (c) Gold at full power is a beacon, so using it risks the ghost, which makes every real fight a decision about his family.
  4. (d) No flaw. He stays untouchable and the only cost is moral, which the Elixir already provides.

Status: OPEN

Q2. What does Jon's release cost him?

Why canon forces it: P-003 counts a release as a power-up, and a release that costs nothing is a switch.
Options:

  1. (a) Physical damage limitation: seven years cold means channelling Tier 4 openly injures him now.
  2. (b) A promise: the suppression is a vow made to someone, so breaking it costs him a relationship rather than a body.
  3. (c) Fear: the blue went wrong once and someone got hurt, so every blade he picks up is a risk he is taking with other people.
  4. (d) No cost, he simply chooses, and the drama is entirely in the choosing.

Status: OPEN

Q3. Does Donte's fire ever hurt Donte?

Why canon forces it: the overflow risk is named in the profile and has never been paid on screen.
Options:

  1. (a) Never. The fire is his, his skin is exempt, and the price is always paid by somebody else, which is its own horror.
  2. (b) The black core is the cost: going black burns him from the inside, so the hottest version of him is the one that shortens him.
  3. (c) Burn-out: after a large output he cannot make a flame for days and is an ordinary thirteen-year-old, which is when the world reaches him.
  4. (d) Leave open for now and decide it at the first present-day pyrokinesis beat.

Status: OPEN

Q4. Uno and Aura: is he on the grid or off it?

Why canon forces it: "CANON: Uno has no Aura" and the successful infusion are both in the same profile and are not reconciled.
Options:

  1. (a) Off, absolutely. "No Aura" stands, and Kane's infusion only explains why the conscience took.
  2. (b) On, secretly. The infusion means Uno carries a Springvale trace, and that is the late-series reveal that makes him hunted and gives him his one earned power-up.
  3. (c) Partial. He has a trace but no tier, which means Sera could feed on him and Kūga could resonate with him. Two specific horrors, both unavailable under (a).

Status: OPEN

Q5. What is the ONE thing Uno does that nobody else does?

Why canon forces it: P-001 and P-002 both fail on a kit assembled from the general idea of a cyborg.
Options:

  1. (a) Adaptive learning as a combat signature: he can perform any move he has watched once, cleanly, and that is his whole kit.
  2. (b) He is the one who cannot be stopped, and the flaw is that he cannot be repaired either.
  3. (c) Something Bupe already has that is not in the files.
  4. (d) Keep the kit generic on purpose and let the apple shrine carry him, accepting that his power is not the memorable part.

Status: OPEN

Q6. What is Uno's permanent flaw?

Why canon forces it: the memory damage constrains what he knows, not what he can do, so P-004 has nothing to hold.
Options:

  1. (a) A reactivation protocol Bio Genetix still owns, so somebody out there can switch him off.
  2. (b) Irreparability: Kane is gone, so every injury is permanent and Uno visibly accumulates damage across four seasons.
  3. (c) Power: he runs down and Donte has to keep him going, which rhymes with the apple and makes the bond mechanical as well as emotional.
  4. (d) None. The memory damage is the only flaw and it stays a story flaw.

Status: OPEN

Q7. What does feeding cost Sera?

Why canon forces it: feeding currently appears free, silent, unlimited and undetectable.
Options:

  1. (a) Nothing. Feeding is free and the only brake is her own refusal to want, which is the whole character.
  2. (b) A clock: she has to feed steadily or forty years arrive at once, so the island is a necessity dressed as a choice.
  3. (c) A visible mark she has to hide, so the Serpent legend has a physical source somebody could eventually photograph.

Status: OPEN

Q8. Face and Donte share a black aura root. How is that separated on screen?

Why canon forces it: the theme is deliberate and the legibility risk is not; black smoke and black flame occupy one visual register.
Options:

  1. (a) Separate them on fire versus smoke and never stage them in the same palette until the reveal.
  2. (b) Lean in. The blur IS the reveal, and the first time an audience cannot tell whose black is whose is the point.
  3. (c) Push Donte's black-and-white aura forward in the present day so father and son never share a frame register even when both are lit.

Status: OPEN

Q9. Jaden and Jon both read as calm Tier 4 blade users. Which way?

Why canon forces it: nothing in the files says the overlap was chosen, and it competes with the burn mark as the identification thread.
Options:

  1. (a) Take blades off Jaden as a style: knives are tools he throws, and bladework belongs to Jon alone.
  2. (b) Keep the overlap and use it as the plant, so the reveal lands on an audience that has already half-confused them.
  3. (c) Give Jaden a non-blade signature: name the calculated ricochet from S1E01 as his move and build the identity there.

Status: OPEN

Q10. What does moving up a tier cost, and does the audience ever see it?

Why canon forces it: P-003 wants the bill watched being paid, and canon names the triggers and stops.
Options:

  1. (a) Nothing formal. Tiers stay description, never mechanic, and the show never prices them.
  2. (b) Each tier has a named price (a night, a year, a life) so the ladder becomes a real threat rather than a label.
  3. (c) Tiers stop being spoken of entirely and the Growth Ladder stays a writers' tool, with visibility doing all the audience-facing work.

Status: OPEN

Q11. Does anyone in the ensemble ever pay the Consequences of Overuse on screen?

Why canon forces it: the consequences exist on paper and no character has paid any price for using power in three shipped episodes.
Options:

  1. (a) Yes, once, on a named character early in Season 2, so the audience learns the cost is real before the stakes rise.
  2. (b) Yes, but only on minor characters, so the leads stay safe and the rule is still demonstrated.
  3. (c) No. The consequences remain a lore fact and the show never spends a scene on them.

Status: OPEN

Q12. The Mandem combined ultimate is undesigned and it is Face's and Jon's only power-up.

Why canon forces it: the emotional peak is locked and the power that lands on it is not.
Options:

  1. (a) Design it now so Seasons 1 to 3 can plant it in fragments.
  2. (b) Hold it deliberately and let it arrive unannounced at the finale.
  3. (c) It is not an attack at all. Three men using the arts correctly is what thickens the seal, so the combined ultimate is the prison being rebuilt, which makes the finale mechanically load-bearing rather than spectacle.

Status: OPEN

Q13. Spike's crimson aura in S1E01.

Why canon forces it: aura colour is the show's primary identity device and this colour belongs to nobody.
Options:

  1. (a) Recolour to gold so it matches the wasp motif and the colour language stays clean.
  2. (b) Keep crimson and establish that aura colour is not identity-locked below Tier 4.
  3. (c) Cut the colour and keep only the heat distortion, which is what Bible section 3 specifies for Tier 3 anyway.

Status: OPEN

Q14. Every climb in the ensemble happened before the series started. Which one gets screen time?

Why canon forces it: P-003 counts the time an audience watches pass, and canon's climbs are all backstory: Tribal's literacy, Jaden arriving finished, Donte's awakening in flashback.
Options:

  1. (a) Donte carries it: the present-day climb belongs to the thirteen-year-old and the adults stay finished on purpose.
  2. (b) Tribal carries it: the last chapter of the Book is read across Seasons 2 and 3, so the audience watches the final stretch of a twenty-year climb.
  3. (c) Nobody: the adults are finished by design, and Kevin's capacity is the only growth the series ever shows.
  4. (d) Uno: the one character with no history gets the only visible growth, which is also the answer to what he does that nobody else does.

Status: OPEN

Q15. Four subjects carry no cost in any of the three places (Jaden, Jon, Sera, Uno). One design or four separate gaps?

Why canon forces it: Jon's choice, Uno's ceiling and Sera's refusal are named limits sitting outside the body, the ability and the mind, and Jaden has nothing named at all; Q2 and Q6 already ask Jon's and Uno's prices, so this asks whether they are settled together.
Options:

  1. (a) One design: unpriced is the point at the top of the cast, Q2 and Q6 are answered "no cost" to match, and the series carries four figures nothing holds.
  2. (b) Four separate gaps: each is settled on its own terms and nothing is decided as a set, so Q2 and Q6 run independently of Jaden and Sera.
  3. (c) Split on role: the three the audience is asked to follow get priced (Jaden, Jon, Uno) and Sera stays unpriced, because a monster is scarier without a bill.
  4. (d) All four get priced, so the ensemble carries no unlimited figure at all.

Status: OPEN

Continuity hooks

One line per obligation this page creates, tagged so a director can grep the tag and get every craft obligation on that character, arc or system.

  • CHAR:Jaden ARC:S1 ARC:S3 Whatever cost Q1 settles on has to be visible before the finale reveal, or Jaden reaches it with nothing he cannot do.
  • CHAR:Jon ARC:S2 The release moment is unlocked and its price is unwritten (Q2); both land in the same scene when it is written.
  • CHAR:Donte ARC:S2 The first present-day pyrokinesis beat is where Q3 gets paid or deferred, so it cannot be staged before the call.
  • CHAR:Uno SYSTEM:Aura The "no Aura" line and the infusion cannot both stand unqualified (Q4); the Uno profile is fixed in whichever direction Bupe rules.
  • CHAR:Uno Q5 and Q6 are one design: the signature and the flaw are the same gap in his kit and are answered together.
  • CHAR:Sera ARC:S4 If feeding gains a cost (Q7), the Serpent reputation and the not-ageing tell have to stay consistent with it from her first appearance.
  • CHAR:Face CHAR:Donte Until Q8 is settled, father and son are not staged in the same black register in the same scene.
  • CHAR:Jaden CHAR:Jon ARC:S1 Until Q9 is settled, any cloaked blade user in Season 1 has to be readable against the burn-mark thread rather than against Jon.
  • SYSTEM:Aura A named price for a tier-up (Q10) applies retroactively to every tier statement in the Bible and the profiles.
  • SYSTEM:Aura ARC:S2 The first on-screen Consequences of Overuse beat (Q11) is the moment the audience learns the cost is real; nothing before it can assume they know.
  • CHAR:Face CHAR:Jon CHAR:Jaden ARC:S3 The combined ultimate (Q12) is the only power-up Face and Jon have, so its design gates every plant laid for it in Seasons 1 to 3.
  • CHAR:Spike EP:S1E01 The crimson aura (Q13) is already shipped, so any recolour is a change to a delivered script and propagates to the book and Reading Edition.
  • SYSTEM:Voodoo Voodoo growth during the series is unwritten; any on-screen climb has to keep the cold, void and silence register that separates it from Aura.
  • SYSTEM:Aura The tier ladder is the only thing in canon a cost could ever be retired on, and its rungs carry no costs; if Q10 gives tiers a price, it applies to every rung and not only the next one.
  • CHAR:Tribal CHAR:Jaden CHAR:Donte CHAR:Uno ARC:S2 ARC:S3 Whichever climb Q14 puts on screen has to begin before the power-up it pays for, so the debt is visible while it is being run up.
  • CHAR:Jaden CHAR:Jon CHAR:Uno CHAR:Sera Four subjects carry no cost in any of the three places (Q15): Jaden has nothing named, and Jon's choice, Uno's ceiling and Sera's refusal all sit outside the body, the ability and the mind. Q15 rules whether they are settled as one design or one at a time, and it is answered with Q2 and Q6 rather than after them.

A-003 The groups against the three villain-group tests

Face's crew; Bio Genetix and the TANCs; the Mandem; the 3 Little Ninjaz; Tribal and Sera

Contents

  1. Findings
  2. Open questions for Bupe
  3. Continuity hooks
  4. Canon dependencies
  5. Canon changes proposed

Findings

Three villain-group tests run over five groups: two of them villain groups, one a corporation with an army, and two hero-side trios included because the tests are about groups and A-002 left its differentiation blurs sitting inside them. AT-RISK means canon does not currently answer what the test asks, never that a locked call is wrong. UNRESOLVED means canon has not yet decided which kind of thing the group is, so the test cannot be run, and every Subject cell names its group first.
