Open questions for Bupe

Thirteen choices. Nothing here carries a recommendation, and the affected findings do not move until Bupe rules.

Q1. Which kind of group is Face's crew?

Why canon forces it: one Bible line names five plus "and others", three have been on the page, and P-005's first test is deciding cast or tide before writing a member.
Options:

  1. (a) A cast: the five named members, each with one defining scene by the end of Season 1.
  2. (b) A small cast of three (Face, Spike, Sleeze) in front of faceless muscle, with Manic and Eve arriving only when their plot job does.
  3. (c) Leave it open until the E3 to E4 outlines.

Status: OPEN

Q2. Does Face's crew get a shared mark?

Why canon forces it: canon gives them none and never says the absence is a choice.
Options:

  1. (a) A garment or symbol, named now.
  2. (b) A palette: black threaded through every member's kit, Face's smoke as the group's colour.
  3. (c) A shadow group by design: no uniform, one small private mark (a tattoo, a ring, a hand sign), silhouettes carry the rest.
  4. (d) None, deliberately, and canon records the choice.

Status: OPEN

Q3. What is Manic's one mark, and when does it land?

Why canon forces it: the second in command of the season's villain organisation has no scene, no line and no act in three episodes.
Options:

  1. (a) The illusionist who makes Kevin doubt what he saw, Season 1 back half.
  2. (b) The one who sees Face's cracks first and hides them, the crew's own Elixir.
  3. (c) Off the page until Season 2.
  4. (d) Fold his job into Tribal and cut him.

Status: OPEN

Q4. Does Eve get a mark before her death?

Why canon forces it: her only designed mark lands in Season 3.
Options:

  1. (a) The rivalry with Hella becomes a plot act: she is how Hella learns something.
  2. (b) She is Face's instrument against Sharon.
  3. (c) A Season 3 payoff, off the page until then.

Status: OPEN

Q5. Do Eve and Sera both keep the snake coding?

Why canon forces it: two snake women across two antagonist groups, and P-007 asks for one visual language per group.
Options:

  1. (a) Keep both and separate them on screen: Eve is many small snakes, Sera is one presence with no snake imagery, the title is only what Danim calls her.
  2. (b) Re-code Eve to another animal.
  3. (c) Make the rhyme deliberate: Sera's line is where Eve's snakes come from (a canon change, and a large one).

Status: OPEN

Q6. What does a TANC look like?

Why canon forces it: the army of the series has no design in any file.
Options:

  1. (a) One shared body across all generations with a generation mark.
  2. (b) A new silhouette per generation with one constant mark drawn from the Bio Genetix slogan typography.
  3. (c) Human-passing, like Uno, so the horror is that you cannot tell.
  4. (d) Brief the Design Depo now.

Status: OPEN

Q7. Which kind of group are the TANCs?

Why canon forces it: not one is named across three seasons, and the Adaptive promise of three personalities names none.
Options:

  1. (a) A faceless tide in Season 1, individuals from Season 2 (the three personalities become three named units, each leaving its own mark), the top 5 named and designed by Season 3.
  2. (b) Faceless throughout: Mitchell, Kane and Lucas are the villains, the machines are weather.
  3. (c) The top 5 are the only individuals, ever.

Status: OPEN

Q8. Is ranked 2 a person or a number?

Why canon forces it: the Sonya chain hangs on its approach and it has no name or design.
Options:

  1. (a) Name and design it now.
  2. (b) Keep the rank as the identity: the horror is that it has no name.

Status: OPEN

Q9. What tells Uno apart from the army on sight?

Why canon forces it: conscience is interior and the audience cannot see it.
Options:

  1. (a) None, deliberately, and the story exploits it: he can be mistaken for them.
  2. (b) One visible tell, Kane's mark or the Springvale green in his eyes.
  3. (c) Decide after Q6.

Status: OPEN

Q10. What are the three burn-mark designs?

Why canon forces it: same placement and method are settled, the three designs are not, and E1 already shows a wrist.
Options:

  1. (a) Three designs matching each colour's meaning, Bupe draws or briefs.
  2. (b) Brief the Design Depo now.
  3. (c) Hold, and keep every wrist shot at a distance until decided.

Status: OPEN

Q11. Does the white thread stay the trio's shared mark?

Why canon forces it: the trio's one shared mark lives in Kevin's Theme Palette rather than the Bible's group entry, and nothing shipped stages it on any of the three.
Options:

  1. (a) Promote it to the Bible's group entry and give each member one white element, including something of Uno's that survives the lab.
  2. (b) Drop it: the trio's unifier is behavioural.
  3. (c) Keep it Kevin-only.

Status: OPEN

Q12. Are Tribal and Sera a group?

Why canon forces it: canon calls it "the voodoo line" and never frames them together.
Options:

  1. (a) Yes, with one shared silhouette element beyond the register, chosen now.
  2. (b) Two solo operators, never framed together until Season 4, the register is the only thing shared.

Status: OPEN

Q13. Whose cold is it?

Why canon forces it: the register is fully specified and identical for both, so a room cannot tell the audience which of them was in it.
Options:

  1. (a) Temperature: Sera's is colder.
  2. (b) Sound: Tribal's silence has a pulse.
  3. (c) Light: Sera's void takes colour first.
  4. (d) No difference, by design: the audience is meant to mistake one for the other until the reveal.

Status: OPEN

Continuity hooks

One line per obligation this page creates, tagged so a director can grep the tag and get every craft obligation on that character, arc or system.

  • CHAR:Manic ARC:S1 Manic's first scene must carry the one act that is his (Q3); a first appearance without it makes the P-005 Manic finding permanent.
  • CHAR:Eve CHAR:Sera ARC:S3 ARC:S4 No snake imagery is drawn for Sera and no serpent title is given to Eve until Q5 is ruled.
  • SYSTEM:TANC ARC:S1 The first TANC on the page sets the design for every TANC after it; no Prototype appears before Q6 is answered or the appearance answers it.
  • SYSTEM:TANC ARC:S3 Ranked 2 cannot be staged in the Sonya chain until Q8 is ruled.
  • CHAR:Uno SYSTEM:TANC Any exterior tell given to Uno must be absent from every other TANC (Q9).
  • CHAR:Jaden CHAR:Jon CHAR:Face ARC:S1 No close shot of a burn mark until the three designs exist (Q10); E1 already describes the mark as small, raised and healed, so the gap is live in a shipped episode.
  • CHAR:Kevin CHAR:Donte CHAR:Uno ARC:S2 The trio's first shared frame either carries the white thread on all three or retires it (Q11).
  • CHAR:Tribal CHAR:Sera ARC:S4 Every cold room before the Season 4 reveal is staged to the Q13 ruling, so the audience is taught one rule.
  • CHAR:Spike ARC:S1 Spike's crimson stays A-002's hook: any recolour propagates to the book and Reading Edition.
  • CHAR:Face ARC:S1 Face's crew gets no uniform, symbol or palette on the page until Q2 is ruled; silhouettes carry the safe house.

Anime Guide roadmap

Curriculum and intake queue.

Contents

  1. How intake is chosen
  2. The curriculum
  3. Queue
  4. Not taking

How intake is chosen

Three rules, all binding.

  1. Ingest a source because it opens a question Hood Ninjaz currently has, never because it is next in the playlist.
  2. At least one source in every four must come from outside the Pavelover channel (a different creator, an author, an editor, an interview or a craft book). One channel is one man's taste, not craft consensus, and corroboration counts source families.
  3. View count is a tiebreak, never a reason.

The curriculum

What a complete library would teach, by topic group, and what each group gives Hood Ninjaz. Groups are the ones the channel catalogue already found (scratchpad\anime-guide\pavelover\catalogue.md, "Proposed topic groups").

Topic groupVideosWhat it gives Hood Ninjaz
Protagonists and power systems3What a lead's own power should do that no side character's power does. The founding source, S-001, sits here
Villains and antagonists6How a villain stays threatening on the page, and how a group of villains stays distinct from one another and from the ninjas
Power systems and abilities (general)9How a whole system, tiers, levels, drawbacks, should be priced and staged so it stays a source of tension rather than a lore document
Fights and combat craft6How a fight communicates a power's rules through staging and blocking, without a line of dialogue explaining it
Strongest / tier and rank tropes6How to write a character at or near the top of the power scale without every fight becoming a foregone conclusion
Speedsters3How a fast power stays tactical on the page rather than reading as a single stat
Worldbuilding, symbolism and craft17The largest group. General craft on making a power system, a piece of magic or a visual motif carry meaning beyond the fight it is used in
Arcs, chapters, endings and legacy7How a story's structure, a first chapter, a final chapter, a season, sets or spends the audience's trust, relevant to how a Hood Ninjaz season should open and close
Side characters and character types4How a side character earns a distinct identity without doing the protagonist's job for him
Manga vs anime adaptation and author craft6What changes and what must never change when the same story is told twice, relevant to any future Hood Ninjaz adaptation choice
Anime/manga reviews and recommendations9Recommendation content, not a craft argument. See Not taking
Franchise-specific (JJK, Blue Lock, Bleach, Gachiakuta, etc.)14Tier lists and franchise reaction content, not a craft argument. See Not taking
Tropes to avoid / misunderstood tropes2Naming a trope that looks well used but is actually being misapplied, useful for auditing Hood Ninjaz's own trope choices before a script ships

Not taking

  • Anime/manga reviews and recommendations (9 videos: "I Read 30 Manga Recommendations... HELP ME", "The Best Underrated Ongoing Manga To Read in 2025" and similar). Recommendation content: which manga to read next, not an argument about how a power, a villain or a story is built. No craft claim to extract.
  • Franchise-specific (JJK, Blue Lock, Bleach, Gachiakuta, etc.) (14 videos: tier lists such as "The Ultimate Bleach ESPADA Tierlist" and "A TIER LIST Of The BEST Fire Characters in Bleach", reaction content such as "Knuckles Rates Bluelock's Team Z" and "I Made The BEST Team In Bluelock"). Ranking and reaction content inside one franchise, not a transferable craft rule. No craft claim to extract.

What this is
The schema layer: templates, naming, links, growth, intake, lint, queries, one load for a labour model. Cap 400 lines, held to its own closed-heading and one-idea-per-section rules

The Anime Guide Protocol

The schema layer: templates, naming, links, growth, intake, lint, queries, one load for a labour model. Cap 400 lines, held to its own closed-heading and one-idea-per-section rules

Contents

  1. What this library is and what binds
  2. Folder map
  3. The header block rule
  4. Filenames
  5. Headings
  6. Links
  7. Vocabularies
  8. Templates
  9. Growth protocol
  10. Intake runbook
  11. Lint and the ship gate
  12. Canned queries
  13. The portability seam
  14. Deliberate divergences

What this library is and what binds

A craft library. Anime and manga craft video essays become source records, source-traced principle pages, case-study pages holding the facts about reference works, and applied pages that test principles against Hood Ninjaz canon. Nothing here binds a script. A principle carries Standing: studied until Bupe ratifies it into Hood Ninjaz Resources/The Writing Laws.md, in his own idiom, dated and attributed. The Guide proposes, The Writing Laws decide. Applied pages read canon and never write it.

Folder map

Anime Guide/: INDEX.md (the map, pointers, five tables, the exceptions register), PROTOCOL.md (this file), ROADMAP.md (curriculum and intake queue), LOG.md (append-only, prefixed, greppable), Principles/ (one file per craft principle), Sources/ (one file per ingested source), Sources/assets/ (frames and stills, Drive only, gitignored, never committed), Case Studies/ (one file per reference work), Applied/ (one file per Hood Ninjaz subject under test), wiki-site/ (the generated browsable site, one committed index.html, rebuilt by tools/anime-guide-site.py and published by tools/publish-wikis.sh, never edited by hand). Flat inside each folder, no sub-foldering until a folder passes about 15 files. No nested CLAUDE.md inside Anime Guide/: it would auto-load and duplicate the workspace rules.

The header block rule

Every page opens with an H1, a blank line, then a block of **Field:** lines, then a --- divider. The header block never exceeds sixteen lines including the H1 and the blank line, so sed -n '1,16p' <file> always returns the whole header. Header fields are bold-label plain lines, not YAML: nothing in this repo parses YAML, and bold labels grep cleanly. Nothing but fields may appear above the divider.

Filenames

ASCII only: letters, digits, spaces, hyphens, parentheses; no apostrophe, ampersand, comma, colon or diacritic (Kuga in a filename, Kūga in prose). Spaces are literal on disk, %20 in links; parentheses stay literal in links. Principle: P-0NN <Short title>.md. Applied: A-0NN <Subject> against <what>.md. Source: YYYY-MM-DD - Channel - Short Title Slug (videoid).md, dated by the source's own upload date, video id included for one-grep lookup. Case study: the work's English title, nothing else. IDs are permanent, assigned once, never renumbered, never reused, gaps expected: P-0NN, S-0NN, A-0NN, CS-<work-slug>, the join key. Tags: closed prefix vocabulary, only in a **Tags:** header line and continuity-hook lines: CHAR:<Name>, ARC:S1 to ARC:S4, EP:S1E01, SYSTEM:<name>, ASCII, diacritics stripped.

Headings

One # H1 per file, matching the filename. ## sections come only from that type's closed list, spelled identically, fixed order, always present; an empty section carries the single line None yet. No ## heading text repeats inside a file, so rg -n "^## " on any file is a table of contents with addresses. ### only for the fixed sub-slots a template names, never for prose structure. One idea per section. Bupe's own hand edits are exempt: if he adds a heading or a line by hand it stays and lint tidies it later, the address space is a tool for models, never a reason to revert the owner.

Links

Relative markdown links, resolved from the linking file's own location, never absolute. %20 for spaces, parentheses literal, external URLs written out in full. No wiki-style [[links]]. No #anchor links into large canon files: pointers into the Series Bible are prose, for example Series Bible section 4, "The Power of Will: The Rules". Video timestamps: [07:18](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=EJN82NpLQf0&t=438s), minutes and seconds visible, seconds in the URL. What may cross a link, and what may not: Principle to Source, timestamp ranges and a one-line paraphrase may cross, the transcript and on-screen notes may not. Principle to Case study, the work's name and its role word may cross, any figure, episode, chapter or date may not. Principle to Applied, character name plus verdict token plus link (5 lines max) may cross, any reasoning, canon quote or number may not. Applied to Canon, the file path and exact heading in quotes plus one short phrase may cross, any canon paragraph or restated rule may not. Applied to Principle, the ID and the rule's one-line form may cross, the test and the evidence may not. Case study to Source, the source ID in a "cited by" column may cross, the source's argument may not. Source to Principle, the ID list in the header may cross, the rule text may not. Every page has an inbound link from INDEX.md; an orphan is a lint failure. Canon links back exactly twice, once from WIKI.md's Craft block and once from The Writing Laws.md's pointer paragraph: craft learns about canon, canon does not learn about craft.

Vocabularies

Three axes, never mixed, plus four closed lists. Standing (does it bind a script, principle pages only): studied (Vision researched it, binds nothing, default for every new principle) or ratified YYYY-MM-DD by Bupe (written into The Writing Laws, binds every draft). Evidence (how strong is the support, principle pages only): SINGLE-SOURCE (one family argues it), CORROBORATED (two-plus independent families, or it survives against the works themselves), CONTESTED (sources disagree, both positions recorded, nobody has ruled, a legitimate long-lived resting state). Status (is the page alive, every page type): LIVE normal, STALE (an applied page whose canon basis has moved since its read stamp, verdicts untrustworthy until re-run), SUPERSEDED by <ID> on YYYY-MM-DD (replaced, body stays as the record), PARKED (deliberately held, with a reason). Finding verdicts (applied Findings table only): PASSES, AT-RISK, UNRESOLVED, COLLISION (contradicts current canon, needs Bupe's call), FIXED YYYY-MM-DD (pointer to the canon file that fixed it). Question status (applied page questions only): OPEN, or DECIDED YYYY-MM-DD plus a pointer to where the answer now lives in canon; the applied page never holds the canon text. Verification verdicts (source verification tables only): VERIFIED, CORRECTED (the truth differs, the correct value is given), UNVERIFIABLE (no objective test exists: an opinion, a craft assertion, or a figure resting on an undefined milestone; tier and URL n/a). Source tiers (verification tables only): PRIMARY, SECONDARY, FAN-WIKI-FETCHED, FAN-WIKI-UNFETCHED; a FAN-WIKI-UNFETCHED fact may never be the sole basis of a craft rule (lint check 6). Queue statuses (ROADMAP only): QUEUED, INGESTED S-0NN, MERGE-ONLY (folded into existing principles, no source page), SKIP with a reason. LOG prefixes (closed): INGEST, PRINCIPLE-NEW, PRINCIPLE-STRENGTHENED, PRINCIPLE-EXTENDED, PRINCIPLE-SPLIT, PRINCIPLE-SUPERSEDED, DISAGREEMENT-OPENED, DISAGREEMENT-RULED, CASE-STUDY-NEW, APPLIED-NEW, APPLIED-STALE, APPLIED-REFRESHED, DECIDED, RATIFIED, LINT. Corroboration counts source families, not files. Two Pavelover videos agreeing is one channel agreeing with itself. A family is a creator, an author or an interviewee. This is a hard rule in PROTOCOL.md, not a note.

Templates

One paragraph, Vision's own formulation, never a quote. The rule as it would be applied to a page of script.

Three to six sentences. The mechanism, not the enthusiasm.

Numbered checks a reader can run against a draft and get a yes or no from.

What it looks like when the rule is broken, named as a symptom a reader would notice.

**ID:** S-001

# The Anime Guide

Craft library. How anime is written at a world-class level, built source by source, tested against Hood Ninjaz.

**Start here if you are writing tonight.** Find your principle in the first table, open it, read the header block.

**What binds writing.** Nothing here binds a script until Bupe ratifies it into [The Writing Laws](../Hood%20Ninjaz%20Resources/The%20Writing%20Laws.md). A principle's `Standing` field says which it is.

**The rest.** Rules and templates: [PROTOCOL.md](PROTOCOL.md). Intake queue: [ROADMAP.md](ROADMAP.md). History: [LOG.md](LOG.md).

# Anime Guide roadmap

**Status:** LIVE
**Channel catalogued:** Pavelover, 92 long-form videos in 13 topic groups, catalogued 2026-09-04
**Ingested:** 1
**Last touched:** 2026-09-04

---

## How intake is chosen

Three rules, all binding.
1. **Ingest a source because it opens a question Hood Ninjaz currently has, never because it is next in the playlist.**
2. **At least one source in every four must come from outside the Pavelover channel** (a different creator, an author, an editor, an interview or a craft book). One channel is one man's taste, not craft consensus, and corroboration counts source families.
3. View count is a tiebreak, never a reason.

## The curriculum

What a complete library would teach, by topic group, and what each group gives Hood Ninjaz.

## Queue

| Order | Source | ID | Topic group | Why it is next | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | How To Write A Protagonist's Powers (& How NOT To) | EJN82NpLQf0 | Protagonists and power systems | Founding source | INGESTED S-001 |

## Not taking

Sources deliberately out of scope, with the reason, so they are not re-proposed.

Growth protocol

A second source never creates a second principle page for the same rule; dedupe before any writing.

SituationWhat happensLog line
AgreesNew ## Evidence row. Sharpen **The rule:** only if clearer. Evidence moves to CORROBORATED only if the new source is a different familyPRINCIPLE-STRENGTHENED
Narrower/broaderRule line rewritten narrower; broader reading recorded under ## DisagreementsPRINCIPLE-EXTENDED
ExtendsNew numbered check or failure mode, plus evidence row. Breaks 120 lines = a separate linked principlePRINCIPLE-EXTENDED
DisagreesBoth positions in ## Disagreements, neither deleted, neither declared right, one line labelled "Vision's read" (a read, never a verdict). Rule line gains (contested, see Disagreements), Evidence becomes CONTESTED, INDEX gains an exceptions rowDISAGREEMENT-OPENED

Why recency does not settle a disagreement. The Leanness Law's "newest dated decision wins" (root CLAUDE.md section 4) governs Vision's own decisions. Two video essayists disagreeing is not a dated decision, it is two opinions, and the newer one is not more authoritative. Treating it as one would silently import the last thing watched as law. A ruling closes a disagreement: appended as **Ruling (Bupe, YYYY-MM-DD):**, the losing position stays as record, logged DISAGREEMENT-RULED. CONTESTED is a legitimate resting state; the library may not quietly pick a side.

Supersession (until Bupe rules, open decision 2): retired file stays in place, Status becomes SUPERSEDED by P-0NN on YYYY-MM-DD, rule line gains the same first phrase, body stays intact, INDEX moves the row to exceptions, every inbound link fixed in the same edit; nothing to BIN/ unless it has no lasting value at all. Split (a principle turns out to be two): original keeps its ID and the narrower half, new half takes the next free ID, both cite the same sources, INDEX and LOG updated together (PRINCIPLE-SPLIT); no parent summary page, that is a copy. Restale: git log --oneline <canon-read-sha>..HEAD -- <each Canon dependencies path>; any output means stale, labour sets Status: STALE, adds the exceptions row, logs APPLIED-STALE, never rewrites the page (refreshing is the director's); reverse, when canon is edited the editor runs rg -l "CHAR:<name>" in Anime Guide/ and marks every hit stale, same edit. Decided question: becomes DECIDED YYYY-MM-DD plus a pointer, the finding becomes FIXED YYYY-MM-DD, logged DECIDED; later canon contradicting it is a COLLISION, to Bupe, never resolved by a model. Stop rules: principle pages capped by novelty not video count (target 30-40 across the channel); a source earns a page only if it produced a new principle or disagreement, else a MERGE-ONLY ROADMAP row; case studies only for works cited by two-plus sources, or named a model/counter-example; applied pages are per subject, not per principle.

Intake runbook

D is the director (the loop, the reasoning, the merge gate). L is labour in a cage: disjoint file ownership (no worktrees on the Drive-hosted repo, no git commands of its own), one job, no canon writes. The director never delegates step 5, step 8 verdicts, or step 10.

#WhoStepOutput
0DPick the next source off ROADMAP by what Hood Ninjaz needs, honouring one-in-four. Claim the workspace, bash tools/vision-sync.sh checkclaim, builder brief
1LDedupe: rg "<video id>" "projects/hood-ninjaz/Anime Guide". A hit stops itnothing
2LFetch into the scratchpad, never the repo: yt-dlp metadata/captions/video, ffmpeg contact sheets then a deep frame passscratchpad only
3LEvidence pass: segment at topic shifts, quote claims, read frames, [ON SCREEN] notes, list every claim. Flag what is not confidently identifiedscratchpad evidence file
4L (different instance)Fact check every claim: VERIFIED/CORRECTED/UNVERIFIABLE, URL and tier, note unfetchable pagesscratchpad fact-check table
5DRead the two tables only. Decide the split: new principles, evidence rows, disagreements, case studies, IDs and slugsinstruction to labour
6LWrite the source page: verification table complete, segment map inside first 60 linesSources/...
7LWrite/update principle and case-study pages to D's split; every claim carries a source ID, timestamp, matches step 4's verdictPrinciples/, Case Studies/
8D, labour mappingThe applied pass: labour may do read-only canon mapping, scoped. Verdicts and questions are the director's. No canon file touchedApplied/
9LRegister: INDEX rows, exceptions, ROADMAP status, LOG lines, same edit as the pagesINDEX, ROADMAP, LOG
10DShip gate, commit and push, inspect the push result rather than assuming itclean tree on main
11DBatch every open question to Bupe as choices, never drippedone batch of choices

Known ingest gotcha, recorded so intake 2 does not rediscover it (from the S-001 ingest, 2026-09-04): the 720p format string failed with HTTP Error 403: Forbidden. Retrying with --extractor-args "youtube:player_client=android,web" succeeded but offered only format 18, a 360p progressive stream. 360p was legible for every on-screen card. Expect the same and do not spend an hour on it. Solved on the S-003 ingest, 2026-09-05: --js-runtimes node returned 720p with no HTTP 403 (yt-dlp needs a JavaScript runtime and node is on the PATH), so try that flag first and keep the 360p fallback. Why no raw material enters the repo. Two reasons, both in PROTOCOL.md. Leanness: the cleaned substance in the source page is the record, and a verbatim transcript beside it is two live versions of the same substance. Copyright: reproducing a fourteen-minute essay wholesale puts a copy of someone else's work in Bupe's repo for no gain. The intel skill bins the raw transcript after cleaning (.claude/skills/intel/SKILL.md step 5); here it never arrives, which is a stricter version of the same rule.

Lint and the ship gate

The lint pass (every fifth ingest, or on demand): 1 orphans, every page reachable from INDEX.md; 2 dead links resolve on disk; 3 stale applied pages via the growth-protocol git command; 4 caps: principle 120, applied 300, case study 200, PROTOCOL 400; 5 aged contests, CONTESTED over 90 days with no ruling attempt (a contest blocking a live decision escalates immediately regardless of age); 6 fan-wiki dependence, any FAN-WIKI-UNFETCHED fact as sole basis of a rule; 7 monoculture, source-family ratio against one-in-four; 8 figures, any reference-work number asserted on more than one page (exempt as provenance, not assertion: a source page's Verification table, its header counts and its [ON SCREEN] quotes, LOG lines, a number inside a work's title (Mob Psycho 100, Reverse: 1999), the template examples in this file, dates and HTTP status codes; director's ruling 2026-09-04); 9 mirror check, every verdict token in a principle's ## In Hood Ninjaz matches the applied Findings table; 10 unverified leakage, rg -n "UNVERIFIED" Principles/ returns nothing; 11 contradictions, two pages asserting different things about the same work; 12 voice, an em-dash-codepoint ripgrep returns nothing. Findings go into INDEX's exceptions register plus one LINT log line; Bupe's hand edits are never findings to revert, they are tidied into the conventions and the log records it.

The ship gate (before every commit touching the guide), five cheap deterministic steps: 1 link resolver, every relative target in every changed file, any miss blocks the commit (python tools/anime-guide-lint.py "projects/hood-ninjaz/Anime Guide" runs it with lint checks 1, 2, 4, 8, 9 and 10 plus the header rule); 2 em dash grep across the folder; 3 header block check, at most sixteen lines ending at ---; 4 verification cross-check, no page states a claim the source's verification table corrected; 5 orphan check, every new page has an INDEX row.

Canned queries

QueryCommand
Everything touching a principle IDrg -l "P-003" "Anime Guide"
Every craft obligation on a character tagrg "CHAR:Kevin" "Anime Guide"
Find a source by its video idrg -l "EJN82NpLQf0" "Anime Guide/Sources"
List a file's headings (a table of contents with addresses)rg -n "^## " "Anime Guide/Principles/P-001 Iconic powers.md"
Staleness check for one applied pagegit log --oneline <canon-read-sha>..HEAD -- "<each Canon dependencies path>"
Voice lint, the em dash codepointrg -n "\x{2014}" "Anime Guide"
Run the whole lintpython tools/anime-guide-lint.py "projects/hood-ninjaz/Anime Guide"

The portability seam

Exactly one layer is Hood Ninjaz specific: Applied/. Principles, sources and case studies carry no Hood Ninjaz canon, only the capped pointer section on principle pages. If a second series ever exists, the move is mechanical: Principles/, Sources/, Case Studies/, PROTOCOL.md, ROADMAP.md, LOG.md and INDEX.md move to references/anime-guide/; Applied/ stays in the Hood Ninjaz workspace; one link-rewrite pass fixes the relative paths, and the A- prefix plus the CHAR: tags make every affected line greppable.

Figures live once, and this is the companion rule. Any number (episode, chapter, date, count, subscriber figure) lives on exactly one page and is referenced everywhere else by link, never by value. For a fact about a reference work that is the case-study page. A principle page may carry the shape of a number ("hundreds of chapters apart") and never the number. Non-numeric verified claims are not figures: a verified claim carrying no number (for example that the Kamehameha is Dragon Ball's signature technique) may sit in a principle's Evidence table with its URL, and does not force a case-study page into existence. The slogan: the principle is the argument, not the arithmetic. Where a figure cannot be verified against a primary or authoritative source, the principle is stated without the number rather than weakened or delayed.

Deliberate divergences

Recorded so nobody "fixes" them later: raw material never enters the repo (a stricter version of the intel skill's bin-the-transcript step 5, plus the copyright reason); the Scrapbook's UNUSED and PLACED are not adopted, because they track whether raw material has been used up, a different axis, and a vocabulary with dead values invites misuse; one standing row in the intel README instead of one per source; the boundary between studied and ratified is deliberately repeated in three places (this file, INDEX.md, every principle header), a small, named exception to pointers-over-copies.

See also

Pavelover - How To Write A Protagonist's Powers (& How NOT To), Pavelover - How To Write A Villain Group (& How NOT To)
